@@19227313 <h> My Say : China a regional driver <p> When the 1970s dawned in China , the devastating decade-long Cultural Revolution was still running its course . Fast forward to today and China 's rapid economic rise has inspired awe and a combination of fear and admiration . <p> China is the world 's largest manufacturer and exporter . In Deloitte 's 2016 Global Manufacturing Competitiveness Index , China was ranked top . While the US remains the world 's largest economy , China is already remaking the landscape of international economics and development , society and , by extension , politics . <p> China 's post-1978 reforms marked the start of a gradual and highly coordinated transition to a socialist market economy . The reforms strongly relied on encouraging exports by attracting foreign direct investments . At the same time , it protected the domestic market through controlled liberalisation . <p> Coincidentally , China 's reforms began when the advanced capitalist world was transiting from state-led national development to a new world order of neoliberal economic globalisation . As the wave of neoliberal policies affecting international trade gathered momentum @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ global supply chain . China successfully rode the neoliberal globalisation wave , and by the mid-1980s , had become the pre-eminent outsourced manufacturing destination . <p> Neoliberalism steadily lost credibility after the global financial crisis ( GFC ) of 2007/08 . Many economists blame neoliberalism for the GFC , which marked the end of the long US boom and interestingly accelerated China 's emergence as a global economic power . <p> The International Monetary Fund , in its October 2016 World Economic Outlook , warned that persistent stagnation in the advanced economies could fuel further anti-trade sentiment . This is precisely what has happened . Deep unease over stagnating living standards in the developed economies in the post-GFC period has led to rising populism and a return to nationalist inward-looking policies . <p> In the Brexit referendum held in June 2016 , England voted to leave . In January 2017 , US president Donald Trump formally scrapped the Trans-Pacific Partnership deal . And at the March 2017 G20 finance summit , ministers dropped their traditional anti-protectionist pledge following pushback from US officials . <p> With the return to nationalist , @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ a further hit . As it is , the pace of integration has already been slowing since the GFC . Global trade growth , which outpaced global GDP growth over the 1990s and 2000s , has been expanding at a slower pace than global output . <p> Harvard University historian Niall Ferguson has argued that there are economic and political parallels between today 's global situation and the world of the early 20th century . Charles Kindleberger , in his book The World in Depression 1929 -- 1939 , blamed the unstable international economic system prevailing then for the 10-year global economic crisis . The British had not been up to the task of stabilising it , while the US was unwilling to assume responsibility . <p> Unfortunately , we are now living in a G-Zero world , according to Eurasia Group president Ian Bremmer , with no one single country or bloc of countries capable of driving a truly international agenda . This is where China , bent on achieving economic hegemony , comes into the picture . At the World Economic Forum in Davos in January 2017 , @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ against blindly pursuing national interests . <p> With the US president 's " America First " policy and Europe preoccupied with its own predicaments , it is not surprising that China wants to take a shot at the global leadership role . China is after all highly exposed to the global economic system and certainly feels it is a victim of it . It has , as part of its grand strategy to achieve economic hegemony , established the Asian Infrastructure Investment Bank and the BRICS New Development Bank , rolled out its " One Belt , One Road " ( OBOR ) initiative and internationalised the renminbi . <p> Many people fear that China will use its financial clout to gain geopolitical subservience , especially in this region . However , it can not be denied that the region will stand to benefit a lot economically from China 's rise , especially if China focuses more on providing regional rather than global economic leadership . After all , in the G-Zero world , the future of the global economy will likely be more about " regionalisation " and less @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ most important trading partner of many economies in this region . For example , the US used to be Malaysia 's largest trading partner ; that position is now occupied by China , and this has been so for eight consecutive years . China is also the largest trading partner of Japan , the world 's third largest economy and one of the US ' closest allies . <p> In the initial decades of China 's reform programme , it benefited from economic ties with the newly industrialised economies ( NIEs ) -- Hong Kong , Singapore , South Korea and Taiwan . The NIEs had provided international market nous , capital , technology and managerial skills . <p> China has come full circle . It is now in a position to provide the region with economic leadership as the world becomes increasingly fragmented . <p> The Asian Development Bank estimates that Southeast Asia will need to spend an equivalent of 5.7% of GDP on infrastructure over the 2016 to 2030 period to maintain growth momentum , eradicate poverty , and respond to climate change . @@19243799 <h> Cover Story : The Commonwealth Connection <p> The friendship between Malaysia and Canada has stood the test of time because of the shared values and ideals that contributed to a 60-year-long partnership . Ahead of Canada Day on July 1 , Kong Wai Yeng speaks to Her Excellency , High Commissioner Judith St George , about the 150th anniversary of Canada 's Confederation , as well as building a firmer relationship with Malaysia that will put both countries on an even stronger footing . <p> A string of " Move-to-Canada " memes spread like wildfire in November last year , when people vowed to move to the North American country following the results of the US election . It was probably meant as a joke , but why would anyone not want to move to the world 's second-largest country , which is endowed with postcard views , jaw-dropping national parks , snow-capped mountains , rugged wilderness and a diverse culture and history ? The temptation to immediately jet off to this land of wonders , for whatever reason , is hard to resist . <p> Malaysia is far @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ in the world : Trudeau and Bieber . We are a 24-hour flight away , or 12,837km to be exact . But Canada and Malaysia , bound by a shared Commonwealth heritage , are closer than you think . <p> " Canada was one of the first countries to recognise and establish diplomatic ties with the newly independent Federation of Malaya in 1957 , " says Her Excellency , High Commissioner Judith St George , during our interview just two weeks before Canada Day on July 1 . The national holiday marks the anniversary of the July 1 , 1867 enactment of the Constitution Act , 1867 ( then called the British North America Act , 1867 ) . Canada became a self-governing dominion of Great Britain and a federation of four provinces : Qu ? bec , Ontario , New Brunswick and Nova Scotia . <p> St George has become a familiar face whenever Canada makes the headlines in Malaysia , be it at the launch of a clean technology ( cleantech ) company in Sarawak or a photo exhibition that speaks out against child marriage . In real @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ grace the local press : warm , friendly and unflappably poised . After all , a cheerful countenance goes a long way , especially in her line of work , strengthening the political , economic and cultural ties between two governments . <p> " We have always had a strong relationship with Malaysia . But this year is special , " says St George . Indeed , 2017 is an auspicious year for both Canada and Malaysia . @@19253339 <h> Half-a-degree of warming boosted extreme weather <p> Half a degree Celsius of global warming has been enough to increase heat waves and heavy rains in many regions of the planet , researchers reported Friday . <p> Comparing two 20-year periods 1960-79 and 1991-2010 between which average global temperatures jumped 0.5 C ( 0.9 F ) , scientists found that several kinds of extreme weather gained in duration and intensity . <p> Half a degree Celsius of global warming has been enough to increase heat waves and heavy rains in many regions of the planet , researchers reported Friday . <p> Comparing two 20-year periods 1960-79 and 1991-2010 between which average global temperatures jumped 0.5 C ( 0.9 F ) , scientists found that several kinds of extreme weather gained in duration and intensity . <p> The hottest summer temperatures increased by more than 1 C ( 1.8 F ) across a quarter of Earth 's land areas , while the coldest winter temperatures warmed by more then 2.5 C ( 4.5 F ) . <p> The intensity of extreme precipitation grew nearly 10 percent across a quarter of all @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ can fuel devastating forest fires lengthened by a week in half of land areas . <p> These changes were well outside the bounds of natural variability , according to the study , published in the journal Nature Climate Change . <p> " We have to rely on climate models to predict the future , " said lead author Carl-Friedrich Schleussner , a researcher at the Potsdam Institute of Climate Impact Research . <p> " But given that we now have observational evidence of around 1 C warming , we can also look at the real-life impacts this warming has brought , " he said in a statement . <p> In science , observed trends are generally seen as more reliable than projections , which can vary sharply depending on the assumptions made . <p> Changes in climate sometimes defined as " average weather " can only be detected across time periods measured in decades or longer . <p> 0.5 C does matter <p> Global warming caused mostly by the burning of fossil fuels began slowly in the early 19th century with the onset of industrialisation , but has accelerated rapidly @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ 196-nation Paris Agreement , inked in the French capital in 2015 , vowed to cap the rise of the planet 's average surface temperature at " well under " 2 C ( 3.6 F ) , and to " pursue efforts " to block it at 1.5 C ( 2.7 C ) . <p> To inform that effort , the Intergovernmental Panel on Climate Change ( IPCC ) -- the UN 's top science advisory group will issue a report for policy makers in September 2018 on the feasibility of the 1.5 C target , and what impacts might be avoided if it is met . <p> The new study one of thousands that will be reviewed by the IPCC suggests even a half degree rise is significant . <p> " With the warming the world has already experienced , we can see very clearly that a difference of 0.5 C really does matter , " said co-author Erich Fischer , a scientist at ETH Zurich in Switzerland . <p> Earlier research based on computer models , also led by Schleussner , concluded that 2 C of warming would compared @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ water shortages and heatwaves in many regions of the world . <p> It also found that holding the rise in temperature to 1.5 C would give coral reefs -- the cornerstone of ecosystems that sustain half-a-billion people and a quarter of marine wildlife a fighting chance of adapting to warmer and more acidic seas . <p> An extra half-a-degree on top , however , would expose most reefs to possible
